**Publication year:** 2020. **Access / attempt date:** 2026-10-13.

**Type:** Primary paper. **Purpose:** Research entry. **Inspection:** Opened.

**Inspected location:** Section 2, equations (3)–(7), Theorem 1

**Supports**

- A likelihood ratio formed using an independent training split gives a finite-sample confidence set.
- Validity relies on the model, density and data-splitting assumptions, not asymptotic regularity.

**Limits:** Journal year is 2020; arXiv first submission was 2019. No claim that arbitrary data-dependent split choices retain validity. Pin the arXiv v4 identifier when citing; generated HTML front-matter dates need not equal the arXiv revision date.

<details>
<summary>SRC-FORMAL-004 · Exploring the entropic region</summary>

[Exploring the entropic region](https://arxiv.org/html/2509.12439v1)

**Authors / responsible organization:** Laszlo Csirmaz.

**Publication year:** 2025. **Access / attempt date:** 2026-10-13.

**Type:** Author survey. **Purpose:** Research entry. **Inspection:** Opened.

**Inspected location:** Sections 2, 6 and 9.5; Definition 24; Lemma 27; Claims 31-32; Problems 2, 6, 7

**Supports**

- Records distinctions among entropic, almost-entropic and polymatroid vectors.
- Provides an explicit entropy-region problem list and copy-lemma methods.
- Definition 24 and Lemma 27 justify a partial conditional copy by restricting a full copy; Claims 31-32 identify conditioning sets that cannot strengthen the Shannon cone.

**Limits:** Problem 6 must be read with the 2026 follow-up SRC-FORMAL-016; listing a question in 2025 does not establish its current status.

**Related subjects:** [Entropy inequalities and information geometry](domains/info.md#info-entropy).

**Authors / responsible organization:** Stanisław P. Radziszowski.

**Publication year:** 2026. **Access / attempt date:** 2026-10-14.

**Supports**

- The four-color triangle threshold is listed as open with bounds 51 and 62.
- The survey distinguishes exact Ramsey values, constructive lower bounds, upper bounds, and incorrect historical claims.
- Selected table entries give gaps for R(3,10), R(3,11), R(3,12) and R(4,6). Table Ib improves the R(4,6) upper bound to 40; Table Ia retains the older 41 entry.

**Limits:** Living URL may change; record revision before each reuse. Selected entries were inspected, not every cited computation. For R(4,6), read Table Ib together with Table Ia rather than treating the earlier table as current on its own.

<summary>SRC-FORMAL-104 · Dense networks that do not synchronize and sparse ones that do</summary>

[Dense networks that do not synchronize and sparse ones that do](https://arxiv.org/html/1906.10627v4)

**Authors / responsible organization:** Alex Townsend; Michael Stillman; Steven H. Strogatz.

**Publication year:** 2020. **Access / attempt date:** 2026-10-14.

**Type:** Primary research preprint, version 4. **Purpose:** Research entry. **Inspection:** Opened.

**Inspected location:** Section II, equations (1), (4), (5); Section II.1 Ring network; Section III.1

**Supports**

- The homogeneous attractive Kuramoto model on a circulant graph has uniformly twisted equilibria with an explicit Jacobian spectrum.
- For a cycle, the spectrum reduces to a cosine factor times the cycle Laplacian spectrum.
- Additional zero eigenvalues can leave nonlinear stability undecided.

**Limits:** Year refers to the inspected 17 June 2020 version. Historical density bounds are not presented as current; the HTML typesetting date is not the research publication date.

**Authors / responsible organization:** Carol Wu; Elina Robeva.

**Publication year:** 2026. **Access / attempt date:** 2026-10-14.

**Supports**

- Theorem 2.5 restates the classical sufficient front-door criterion and identification functional.
- The paper supplies weaker sufficient conditions; the classical criterion is not necessary for validity of the same functional.

**Limits:** The classical criterion is used for calibration; the completeness question is indexed separately. The newer proofs have not been independently verified. Strict positivity of the observed joint distribution is a standing assumption. Original credit for the classical result belongs to Judea Pearl.

- Each agent's positive pair a_i>b_i>0 defines personalized bi-valued goods.
- EFX existence is proved; simultaneous EFX and Pareto optimality, and its polynomial-time computation, are posed as open.

**Limits:** The separate existence of EFX and Pareto-optimal allocations does not prove simultaneous existence. Pareto testing is hard for general fractional ratios.

**Related subjects:** [Mechanism design and fair allocation](domains/econ.md#econ-mech).

**Connection to these subjects:** Definitions, existence boundaries and algorithmic questions in fair allocation.

**Indexed tasks using this source**

- [EFX and Pareto optimality for personalized bi-valued goods](cards/Q-MODEL-204.md)
- [Exact EFX for common three-value additive goods](candidates/Q-MODEL-206.md)

**Limits:** The underlying DNS data were not publicly deposited; access requires a separate request. The published one-transverse-component relation uses 4/15, correcting the older arXiv:2601.12528v1 wording. Finite-Reynolds-number agreement is not an asymptotic proof.

**Related subjects:** [Fluid and plasma theory](domains/phys.md#phys-fluid), [Statistical and nonequilibrium physics](domains/phys.md#phys-stat).

<a id="src-impact-001"></a>

<details>
<summary>SRC-IMPACT-001 · Stronger Lower Bounds for (Non-)Anytime Acceleration of Gradient Descent</summary>

[Stronger Lower Bounds for (Non-)Anytime Acceleration of Gradient Descent](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.04032v2)

**Authors / responsible organization:** Minchan Jung; Hanseul Cho; Chulhee Yun.

**Publication year:** 2026. **Access / attempt date:** 2026-10-20.

**Type:** Author preprint. **Purpose:** Research entry. **Inspection:** Opened.

**Inspected location:** Version 2, 4 September 2026; equation (1), Theorems 1.1-1.2 and Sections 3-5. Version date checked against the arXiv abstract page.

**Supports**

- Defines the dimension-uniform, normalized last-iterate objective gap.
- Reports a fixed-horizon lower bound with exponent log_2(1+sqrt(3)) and a distinct anytime limsup barrier.
- Section 5 leaves the upper/lower rate gap unresolved.

**Limits:** The stated lower bounds were read, not independently proved. The anytime theorem is a limsup obstruction, not a pointwise lower bound at every iteration.

**Related subjects:** [Optimization and operations research](domains/math.md#math-opt), [Algorithms and data structures](domains/cs.md#cs-alg).

**Connection to these subjects:** Worst-case convergence and deterministic stepsize algorithms.

**Indexed tasks using this source**

- [Optimal acceleration from predetermined gradient-descent stepsizes](cards/Q-FORMAL-510.md)

[Structured source data](../data/sources.json) · [Back to the directory](#source-directory)

[Acceleration by stepsize hedging: Silver Stepsize Schedule for smooth convex optimization](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10107-024-02164-2)

**Authors / responsible organization:** Jason M. Altschuler; Pablo A. Parrilo.

**Publication year:** 2025. **Access / attempt date:** 2026-10-20.

**Type:** Journal article. **Purpose:** Research entry. **Inspection:** Opened.

**Inspected location:** Mathematical Programming 213, 1105-1118; published online 25 November 2024, issue September 2025; DOI 10.1007/s10107-024-02164-2; Sections 1-3.

**Supports**

- Gives a deterministic silver stepsize schedule with objective-gap exponent log_2(1+sqrt(2)) at its terminal iterations.
- Explains recursive composition of multi-step descent inequalities.

**Limits:** This is an upper-bound construction, not a proof that its rate is optimal. Special terminal iterations and anytime guarantees must be distinguished.

**Related subjects:** [Optimization and operations research](domains/math.md#math-opt), [Algorithms and data structures](domains/cs.md#cs-alg).

[Anytime Acceleration of Gradient Descent](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v291/zhang25a.html)

**Authors / responsible organization:** Zihan Zhang; Jason Lee; Simon Du; Yuxin Chen.

**Publication year:** 2025. **Access / attempt date:** 2026-10-20.

**Type:** Conference proceedings. **Purpose:** Research entry. **Inspection:** Opened.

**Inspected location:** COLT 2025, PMLR 291:5991-6013; conference 30 June-4 July 2025; publisher abstract and bibliography.

**Supports**

- Reports one predetermined infinite schedule with a last-iterate guarantee for every stopping time.
- Its exponent is 2a/(1+a), where a=log_2(1+sqrt(2)); it resolves the earlier question of beating 1/T in the anytime setting.

**Limits:** Publisher abstract inspected; the full proof was not audited. The sharp anytime exponent remains a separate question.

**Related subjects:** [Optimization and operations research](domains/math.md#math-opt), [Algorithms and data structures](domains/cs.md#cs-alg).

[When Maximum Nash Welfare Becomes Strongly Fair: Bi-valued Goods](https://arxiv.org/html/2610.00122v1)

**Authors / responsible organization:** Zehan Lin; Xiaowei Wu; Shengwei Zhou.

**Publication year:** 2026. **Access / attempt date:** 2026-10-20.

**Type:** Author preprint. **Purpose:** Research entry. **Inspection:** Opened.

**Inspected location:** Version 1; Theorem 3.5, Example 3.6 and Section 6. The arXiv abstract and HTML header report 9 September 2026, while the manuscript title page says December 2025; the date discrepancy is retained.

**Supports**

- For personalized bi-valued goods, maximum Nash welfare allocations have a tight 2/3-EFX guarantee.
- This is a guarantee for a particular allocation rule, not a negative answer to simultaneous exact EFX and Pareto optimality.

**Limits:** Preprint; proof not independently audited. Publication chronology needs clarification. Tightness for maximum Nash welfare does not exclude another exact EFX and Pareto-optimal allocation.
